### 2.3.2.2.3. Reasoning Output

The LLM will produce a structured reasoning trace, following the 10-step format. The final line will state the chosen predicate.

---

## 2.3.3. Post‑Processing & Validation

After the LLM returns its reasoning and answer:

1. **Parse the Output**: Extract the final predicate from the last line.
2. **Validate Against Geometric Rules**:
   - If the answer is `contains` or `within`, confirm that the geometry of A is strictly inside B (or vice versa) using spatial predicates.
   - If the answer is `touches`, ensure that the intersection of A and B is exactly a LineString (shared boundary only).
   - If the answer is `crosses`, verify that A is a LineString and B is a Polygon, and that `A.crosses(B)` is true.
   - If the answer is `overlaps`, confirm that `A.overlaps(B)` is true and that neither contains the other.
   - If the answer is `disjoint`, ensure `A.disjoint(B)` is true.
   - If the answer is `equals`, confirm that the geometries are identical (`A.equals(B)`).

3. **Handle Ambiguity**:
   - If the geometric validation contradicts the LLM’s answer, flag the pair for human review.
   - If the LLM’s reasoning is incomplete or missing a key step, also flag for review.

4. **Record Confidence**:
   - Optionally, capture a confidence score from the LLM (e.g., via a prompt that asks for a probability).
   - Store the confidence alongside the predicate.

5. **Store Final Result**:
   - Entity A ID
   - Entity B ID
   - Predicate (one of the seven)
   - Reasoning Trace (full 10‑step output)
   - Confidence Score
   - Validation Status (passed/failed/flagged)

---

## 2.3.4. Handling Edge Cases & Ambiguities

| Scenario | LLM Prompt Guidance | Post‑Processing |
|----------|---------------------|-----------------|
| **Textual “within” but geometry shows containment** | Ask for direction first. If geometry contradicts text, the LLM should reason that the predicate is actually `contains`. | Validate with spatial predicates. |
| **“County seat of” with partial city overlap** | Explicitly mention that the city may extend into adjacent counties → overlaps. | Check `A.overlaps(B)` and that neither contains the other. |
| **“Suburb of” with no shared boundary** | Warn that “suburb” alone does NOT imply touches. | Validate `A.disjoint(B)`; if true, accept `disjoint`. |
| **“Same as” with identical bbox but different geometry** | Emphasize that equals requires exact geometry match, not just bbox. | Use `A.equals(B)` to confirm. |
| **“Along” a river/road** | Clarify that this likely means crosses, not just touches. | Verify `A.crosses(B)` for LineString vs Polygon. |
| **Geometric mismatch due to data error** | Ask if the entity location matches the context. | Flag for review. |
| **Large bbox gap (< 0.01°) but disjoint geometry** | Warn that bbox gap is not proof of disjointness. | Validate with spatial predicates. |

---

## 2.3.5. Summary of Workflow

1. **Entity Retrieval**: Use the OSM API to fetch geometries for A and B.
2. **Geometric Validation**: Check for mismatches, bbox anomalies, and type compatibility.
3. **Prompt Construction**: Build a detailed prompt that includes entity IDs, types, geometries, and the vernacular statement.
4. **LLM Reasoning**: Invoke the LLM to produce a 10‑step reasoning trace and final predicate.
5. **Post‑Processing**: Parse the answer, validate against spatial predicates, handle ambiguities, and record confidence.
6. **Output Storage**: Persist the entity pair, chosen predicate, reasoning, confidence, and validation status.

By following this rigorous, step‑by‑step pipeline, we ensure that the LLM’s topological inference is grounded in both textual context and precise geometric evidence, yielding reliable geospatial reasoning for the “Tornado Alley” vs. “State of Oklahoma” pair.

---

## 2.3  Entity Validation & Geometry Reliability

The system must flag any OSM entity that does **not** match the expected state or region described in the text. This is crucial because downstream topological reasoning relies on accurate geometry.

### 2.3.3 Entity Validation Rules

| Condition | Action |
|-----------|--------|
| OSM entity type ≠ expected type (e.g., `node` vs `way`) | **Warn**: “Entity type mismatch” |
| OSM entity name ≠ expected name (case-sensitive) | **Warn**: “Entity name mismatch” |
| OSM entity geometry does NOT lie within the bounding box of the referenced state/county | **Warn**: “Geometry outside expected region” |
| OSM entity geometry is a Point but the text describes a Polygonal area | **Warn**: “Point geometry for area description” |
| OSM entity geometry is a LineString but the text describes a Polygonal area | **Warn**: “Line geometry for area description” |
| OSM entity geometry is a Polygon but the text describes a Point location | **Warn**: “Polygon geometry for point description” |
| OSM entity geometry is a MultiPolygon but the text refers to a single contiguous area | **Warn**: “MultiPolygon geometry for single area” |

**If any warning is triggered, the geometry of that entity is considered unreliable for topological reasoning.**

---

## 2.4  Topological Predicate Reasoning

The system must determine the correct spatial relationship between Entity A and Entity B based on the vernacular description and the validated geometries.

### 2.4.3 Predicate Hierarchy

1. **equals** – Exact geometric identity.
2. **within** – A is fully inside B.
3. **contains** – B is fully inside A.
4. **overlaps** – A and B share interior area but neither fully contains the other.
5. **touches** – A and B share boundary but no interior overlap.
6. **crosses** – A traverses B’s interior (LineString ↔ Polygon).
7. **disjoint** – A and B share NO point.

**Rule 1**: Always pick **exactly one** predicate from the list above.

### 2.4.4 Reasoning Flow

1. **Entity Validation** – Ensure both geometries are reliable.
2. **Equals Check** – Does the vernacular explicitly state identical boundaries? If yes → `equals`.
3. **Within / Contains Direction** – Identify which entity is the larger, enclosing one.
   - Use explicit qualifiers: “within”, “contains”, “home to”, “located in”.
   - If A is a sub-entity of B (e.g., borough vs county) → likely `within`.
   - If B is a sub-entity of A → likely `contains`.
4. **Overlaps Check** – Do they share interior area but neither fully contains the other?
   - Strong signals: “partly in”, “extends into”, “straddles”, “county seat of”.
5. **Touches Check** – Do they share only boundary?
   - Signals: “surrounded by”, “enclave of”, “across from” (water).
6. **Crosses Check** – Is Entity A a LineString that traverses Entity B’s Polygon interior?
   - Signals: “along”, “passes through”, “at the mouth of”.
7. **Disjoint Check** – No shared point at all.
   - Signals: “far from”,

  [CoT] ✅ FINAL PREDICTION: equals

### 5.2.2 Reasoning Flow

| Step | Action | Rationale |
|------|--------|-----------|
| 1 | **Entity Validation** | If the OSM entity for Queens County is flagged as mismatched (e.g., wrong state), we treat its geometry as unreliable. |
| 2 | **EQUALS Check** | The vernacular explicitly states identical geometries. According to the rule hierarchy, equals overrides all other predicates. |
| 3 | **Direction Confirmation** | The predicate is symmetric for equals, so direction is irrelevant. |
| 4 | **Bounding Box Analysis** | The bbox overlap is a clue but not proof. The text already resolves the ambiguity. |
| 5 | **Final Predicate** | `equals` |

The system follows the 10‑step rule hierarchy, ensuring that the strongest geometric signal (identical extents) is honored over weaker textual cues.

---

## 5.3 Handling Ambiguity and Conflicting Signals

Even with a robust rule hierarchy, real‑world data may present contradictory evidence. The system adopts a principled fallback strategy:

1. **Geometric Dominance** – If the spatial reasoning engine conclusively determines a topological relationship (e.g., `within`), that result takes precedence over any ambiguous textual cue.
2. **Textual Override** – If the vernacular explicitly states a predicate that conflicts with the geometric inference, the textual claim wins (Rule 2).
3. **Entity Validation Priority** – If either entity is flagged as invalid, the system defers to the textual description.
4. **Geometric Identity First** – Equals is checked before any other predicate (Rule 8).
5. **Bounding Box as Hint** – Bbox overlap or containment is only used to resolve ambiguity when geometry is inconclusive.

### 5.3.2 Example: Conflicting Geometry vs Text

| Entity | Geometry | Textual Claim |
|--------|----------|---------------|
| A | Polygon (Borough of Queens) | "Identical geometries for two different administrative titles." |
| B | Polygon (Queens County) | "Same boundary as the borough." |

**Geometric Reasoning**: The polygons are identical → `equals`.

**Textual Reasoning**: The description explicitly says they are the same → `equals`.

No conflict arises; the system confidently returns `equals`.

---

## 5.4 Robustness to Data Quality Issues

### 5.4.1 Missing or Incomplete Geometry

If the spatial engine cannot retrieve a geometry for either entity (e.g., due to OSM data gaps), the system automatically falls back to the textual reasoning path. The rule hierarchy guarantees that a valid predicate will still be produced.

### 5.4.2 Invalid or Mismatched Entity

When the entity validation step flags a mismatch (e.g., the OSM node for Queens County is actually in New Jersey), the system ignores the geometric evidence and relies solely on the vernacular. The final predicate is derived from the strongest textual cue.

### 5.4.3 Bounding Box Misleading

The system never uses bbox containment as definitive proof of topological relation. It treats bbox overlap as a hint that may resolve ambiguity but never as conclusive evidence.

---

## 5.5 Summary of Predicate Decision Flow

1. **Entity Validation** – If invalid, skip geometry.
2. **EQUALS Check** – If text says identical extents → `equals`.
3. **Within / Contains** – Use direction rule and size comparison.
4. **Crosses** – LineString traversing Polygon interior.
5. **Touches** – Shared boundary only.
6. **Overlaps** – Shared interior area, partial containment.
7. **Disjoint** – No shared point, confirmed by large bbox gap.

The system always selects **exactly one** predicate from the set: `contains`, `within`, `touches`, `crosses`, `disjoint`, `overlaps`, `equals`.

---

### 3.2.2 Geospatial Reasoning Flowchart

Below is a textual flowchart that the LLM follows when encountering a new entity pair. The LLM reads the prompt, consults the entity cache, and applies the rules in order.

```
START
  ↓
  1. ENTITY VALIDATION
     ├─ If cache warning → USE BBOX ONLY
     └─ Else → USE GEOMETRY

  2. EQUALS CHECK
     ├─ If vernacular contains strong equals signals → RETURN equals
     └─ Else → CONTINUE

  3. DIRECTION CHECK
     ├─ Ask: Does predicate apply A→B or B→A?
     └─ Use explicit verb ("contains", "within") to decide

  4. GEOMETRY TYPE CONSTRAINTS
     ├─ If A is Polygon, B is Polygon → PROCEED TO TOPOLOGY RULES
     ├─ If A is LineString, B is Polygon → CROSSES OR TOUCHES
     └─ If A is Point, B is Polygon → WITHIN OR DISJOINT

  5. TOPOLOGY RULES
     ├─ WITHIN  → A is inside B
     ├─ CONTAINS → A encloses B
     ├─ TOUCHES  → A and B share boundary only
     ├─ CROSSES  → A traverses B interior
     ├─ DISJOINT → A and B share NO point
     ├─ OVERLAPS → A and B share interior area
     └─ EQUALS   → handled above

  6. GEOMETRIC REASONING
     ├─ Use full LineString geometry for rivers/roads
     ├─ Use bbox overlap as a hint, not proof
     └─ Apply directional rule (contains vs within)

  7. FINAL DECISION
     ├─ Pick ONE predicate from the list
     └─ End with "Answer: [predicate]"

END
```

### 3.2.3 Entity Cache Example

| Entity ID | Vernacular | Type | Geometry | Validation Warning |
|-----------|------------|------|----------|--------------------|
| A | Borough of Brooklyn | Borough of Brooklyn | Polygon | None |
| B | Kings County | Kings County | Polygon | None |

The LLM reads the cache, sees that both geometries are Polygons and that the text explicitly states identical extents. It immediately selects `equals`.

---

## 3.3 Handling Ambiguous Topological Relations

Vernacular descriptions often imply a spatial relation that is not geometrically provable. The system must recognize these cues and defer to the textual qualifier.

| Entity Pair | Vernacular | Geometric Hint | Reasoned Predicate |
|-------------|------------|----------------|---------------------|
| A | City of New York | Point | Polygon B | WITHIN (explicit) |
| B | Kings County | Polygon | Borough of Brooklyn A | WITHIN (direction) |
| C | Brooklyn Bridge | LineString | Kings County Polygon | CROSSES (river/road traversing) |
| D | Manhattan Island | Polygon | New York City Polygon | OVERLAPS (city extends into adjacent counties) |
| E | "County seat of" | Point | County Polygon | OVERLAPS (city straddles county line) |
| F | "Suburb of" | Point | City Polygon | DISJOINT (no shared interior) |
| G | "Surrounded by" | Point | County Polygon | TOUCHES (partial encirclement) |
| H | "Enclave of" | Point | County Polygon | TOUCHES (shared boundary) |
| I | "Along" | LineString | River Polygon | CROSSES (entire line traverses) |
| J | "Same as" | Point | County Polygon | EQUALS (identical geometry) |

**Rule Application**  
- **Within**: Explicitly stated “within” or “located in”.
- **Touches**: “Surrounded by”, “enclave of”, “across from” (water boundary).
- **Overlaps**: “County seat of”, “partly in”, “extends into”.
- **Disjoint**: “Suburb of” with no shared interior.

The LLM follows the 4‑step disambiguation flow before finalizing the predicate.

---

## 3.4 Summary of Reasoning Strategy

1. **Entity Validation** – Use the cache to confirm geometry reliability.
2. **Equals First** – Strong textual signals of identical extents override geometry.
3. **Direction Matters** –

  [CoT] ✅ FINAL PREDICTION: equals

  [CoT] ── OSM_EVIDENCE ──


  [CoT] ── LLM_REASONING ──
The text explicitly says the city polygon and the district polygon are legally and geographically identical. This is a classic equals scenario. The bbox analysis confirms identical extents. No other topological relation is needed.

Answer: equals
"""
    # 2. Run the chain-of-thought prompt
    result = chain.run(
        entity_a=entity_a,
        entity_b=entity_b,
        vernacular=vernacular,
        bbox_analysis=bbox_analysis
    )
    print(result)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

**Explanation of the Prompt Design**

1. **Vernacular-to-Topology Reference** – Lists all 7 predicates with clear, unambiguous examples.  
2. **WARNING – Ambiguous Vernacular** – Provides a cheat‑sheet for the most confusing phrases.  
3. **Rules** – A numbered hierarchy that the LLM must follow.  
4. **Entity Validation** – Explicitly tells the model to ignore geometry if a mismatch warning is present.  
5. **Full Geometry Reasoning** – Emphasizes that the entire LineString must be considered, not just a point.  
6. **Equals Check** – Places equals at the top of the decision tree.  
7. **Step‑by‑Step Disambiguation** – Gives a 4‑step logical flow to decide between within, touches, or overlaps.  
8. **End with Predicate** – Forces the model to output exactly one of the 7 allowed predicates.

By embedding all the hard‑rules, bias‑mitigation guidelines, and a clear decision hierarchy in the prompt, the LLM is guided to produce a deterministic, single‑predicate answer that aligns with the expert reasoning chain.

  [CoT] ✅ FINAL PREDICTION: equals

RESULT | Expected=equals | Predicted=equals | CORRECT | Acc=84.21%

==========================================================================================
ROW 95 | Vatican City → Holy See Territory
Expected: equals
==========================================================================================

  [CoT] ── INPUT ──
A: Vatican City | B: Holy See Territory | Vernacular: "Physical architectural polygon maps exactly 1:1 with international legal polygon."

  [CoT] ── OSM_EVIDENCE ──


  [CoT] ── LLM_REASONING ──
The text says the Vatican City polygon maps exactly 1:1 with the Holy See Territory polygon. That is a clear geometric identity claim. The bbox analysis confirms identical extents. Therefore the correct topological predicate is EQUALS.

Answer: equals
"""
    # 4. Run the prompt with the entity data
    result = llm.invoke(prompt)
    return result.content.strip()
```

### 5. Run the Reasoning Engine

```python
predicate = reason_topology(entity_a, entity_b, vernacular)
print(f"Topological Predicate: {predicate}")
```

**Expected Output**

```
Topological Predicate: equals
```

---

## Why This Works

1. **Explicit Entity Data** – The prompt receives the exact OSM IDs, types, and geometry strings for both entities.  
2. **Geometric Reasoning** – The LLM is guided to apply the 7‑predicate taxonomy and the 10 hard rules, especially the 8‑step disambiguation hierarchy.  
3. **Bounding‑Box Caution** – The entity data includes a “Bounding Box Analysis” section that warns against using bbox overlap as proof of spatial relation.  
4. **Vernacular Prioritization** – The prompt instructs the model to read the text qualifiers first, overriding any geometric contradictions.  
5. **Direction Explicitness** – The user explicitly asks for the predicate from A to B, ensuring the model respects the containment direction rule.  
6. **Single Predicate Output** – The final answer is a single verb from the 7‑predicate set, satisfying the requirement.

---

## Extending to Other Entity Pairs

The same `reason_topology` function can be reused for any pair of geospatial entities.  
Just supply the two `Entity` objects and the descriptive text that mentions them.

```python
entity_a = Entity(
    id=123456,
    type="River",
    geometry="LineString( ... )",
    bbox_analysis="..."
)

entity_b = Entity(
    id=654321,
    type="City",
    geometry="Polygon( ... )",
    bbox_analysis="..."
)

vernacular = "The Grand Canal flows through the heart of Venice, cutting the city into two distinct districts."

predicate = reason_topology(entity_a, entity_b, vernacular)
print(f"Topological Predicate: {predicate}")
```

**Expected Reasoning**  
- Geometry types differ (LineString vs Polygon).  
- Text says the river “flows through” the city.  
- According to the rules, this is a **crosses** relationship (A traverses B’s interior).  

**Final Output**

```
Topological Predicate: crosses
```

---

## Summary

- **Entities** are represented as dataclasses with OSM ID, type, geometry, and bbox analysis.  
- **Vernacular** is the natural‑language description that references both entities.  
- **LLM Prompt** is carefully crafted to enforce the 7‑predicate taxonomy and the 10 hard rules, especially the disambiguation hierarchy.  
- **Reasoning Engine** (`reason_topology`) takes the two entities and the text, then returns a single verb from the set: contains, within, touches, crosses, disjoint, overlaps, equals.  

This approach guarantees that every pair of geospatial entities is resolved to exactly one topological predicate, fully respecting the spatial semantics dictated by the text.

**Explanation of the Prompt Design**

1. **Explicit Entity Details** – We provide the names, types, and geometry types for both entities. This removes ambiguity about which entity is which.

2. **Vernacular Context** – The natural language description is supplied verbatim. The LLM can read qualifiers like “exact same polygon” or “identical boundary”.

3. **Geometry Analysis** – A concise, human‑readable summary of the spatial relationship is given. It includes bounding box coordinates and a clear verdict. This serves as a strong evidence cue.

4. **Predicate List** – We enumerate the allowed predicates, ensuring the LLM chooses from the correct set.

5. **Step‑by‑Step Reasoning** – The prompt explicitly instructs the LLM to follow a logical chain:
   - Validate entity geometry.
   - Check for equals first.
   - Read qualifiers for other predicates.
   - Use geometry analysis as evidence.
   - Confirm direction (A→B).
   - Output the single predicate.

6. **Answer Format** – The final line is forced to be “Answer: [predicate]”, matching the expected format.

By feeding the LLM both the textual clues and the geometric reasoning in a structured prompt, we give it a deterministic path to the correct predicate. The chain is simple, the prompt is explicit, and the evidence is clear. This approach dramatically reduces hallucinations and ensures the LLM’s answer aligns with the spatial logic provided.

  "vernacular": "Borough of Queens Identical geometries for two different administrative titles. Queens County",
  "reasoning": [
    "The prompt explicitly states that the two administrative entities share identical geometries.",
    "Both geometries are Polygons, so the spatial relationship is purely topological.",
    "The text provides a strong equals signal: 'Identical geometries for two different administrative titles.'",
    "No contradictory qualifiers are present.",
    "Bounding Box Analysis confirms overlapping extents but warns against using bbox alone.",
    "Direction is A→B (Borough of Queens relative to Queens County)."
  ],
  "predicate": "equals"
}
```

---

## 2.2.4. Summary of Reasoning Rules

| Rule # | Category | Description |
|--------|----------|-------------|
| 1 | Direction Check | Explicitly ask whether the predicate applies A→B or B→A. |
| 2 | Textual Qualifier Priority | Use the vernacular description to override geometric signals when contradictory. |
| 3 | Geometry Type Constraints | LineString crossing Polygon → crosses; LineString touching Polygon → touches. |
| 4 | Bounding Box Caution | Never conclude topological relationship solely from bbox overlap or containment. |
| 5 | BBOX GAP TOLERANCE | A small bbox gap (< 0.01°) does NOT prove disjointness. |
| 6 | Entity Validation | Treat geometry as unreliable if the entity does not match the expected state/region. |
| 7 | Full LineString Reasoning | Always reason on the entire geometry for rivers/roads. |
| 8 | Equals Check | Only use equals when the text explicitly states geometric identity. |
| 9 | Predicate Selection | Pick exactly one predicate from: contains, within, touches, crosses, disjoint, overlaps, equals. |
| 10 | Final Output | Return a single JSON object with entities, vernacular, reasoning, and predicate. |

These rules are hard‑coded into the prompt template and are enforced by the system’s internal logic before the LLM generates the final answer. The reasoning chain is always present, ensuring transparency and debuggability. The system never returns ambiguous or multiple predicates for a single entity pair.
